1: # Homies Tasks API — Quick Reference (for calendar integration)
2: 
3: Base URL (dev): `http://localhost:8080` · All endpoints require `Authorization: Bearer <jwt>`.
4: Rate limit: 10 req/s on `/lists` and `/user`. Errors always look like:
5: `{"error": {"message": "...", "code": "<error_code>"}}` with HTTP 400 (bad input/DB) or 403 (list belongs to another house).
6: 
7: ## Data model
8: 
9: **List**
10: ```json
11: { "id": "12", "name": "Groceries" }
12: ```
13: 
14: **Item (task)**
15: ```json
16: {
17:   "id": "34",
18:   "text": "Buy milk",
19:   "completed": false,
20:   "author": "uuid-of-creator",
21:   "created_at": "2026-11-10T15:00:00Z",
22:   "due_at": "2026-11-15T18:00:00Z"
23: }
24: ```
25: - `due_at` is **optional**: an RFC3339 UTC timestamp, or `null` when the task has no due date. Always present in responses.
26: - `id`s are strings (stringified ints).
27: 
28: ## Endpoints
29: 
30: ### 1. `GET /lists/` — all lists of the user's house
31: Response: `{"lists": [List, ...]}`
32: 
33: ### 2. `GET /lists/:id/` — items of a list (paged, time-filtered)
34: Query params (all optional, combinable):
35: - `limit` — max items, default **10**, capped at 10
36: - `from` — RFC3339; only items with `created_at >= from`
37: - `to` — RFC3339; only items with `created_at < to`
38: - `due_on` — `YYYY-MM-DD`; only items whose `due_at` falls in that **UTC day**, i.e. `[YYYY-MM-DD 00:00:00Z, next day 00:00:00Z)`. Items with `due_at == null` are excluded. Invalid format → param silently ignored.
39: 
40: ⚠️ Timezone note: `due_on` matches on the **UTC** day, not the device's local day.
41: For a local-time calendar, either accept the UTC-day approximation or compute the
42: local day's UTC boundaries yourself and use `from`/`to`... note however `from`/`to`
43: filter `created_at`, not `due_at` — so for exact local-day filtering, fetch with
44: `due_on` for the UTC day before, the day itself, and the day after (or just the day
45: itself if tasks are due during daytime) and filter `due_at` client-side.
46: 
47: Response: `{"items": [Item, ...]}` — ordered by `created_at` **DESC** (newest first).
48: 
49: **Calendar example:** tasks due on Nov 15, 2026:
50: `GET /lists/34/?due_on=2026-11-15`
51: 
52: ### 3. `PUT /lists/:id/` — create item
53: Body:
54: ```json
55: { "text": "Buy milk", "due_at": "2026-11-15T18:00:00Z" }
56: ```
57: - `text`: required, 3–511 chars.
58: - `due_at`: optional. Omit or send `null` → no due date. Must be RFC3339.
59: 
60: Response: `{"id": "34"}` (id of the new item)
61: 
62: ### 4. `PATCH /lists/:id/:item_id` — update item
63: Body fields are all optional, patch-like:
64: ```json
65: { "text": "New text", "completed": true, "due_at": "2026-11-20T09:00:00Z" }
66: ```
67: Branching logic (quirky, follow exactly):
68: - **`text` non-empty** → updates `text` (+ `due_at` if present, see below). `completed` is IGNORED on this branch.
69: - **`text` empty/absent** → sets `completed` to the given value (+ `due_at` if present).
70: 
71: `due_at` semantics:
72: - field **absent** → existing due date unchanged
73: - field **`null`** → due date removed
74: - field **RFC3339 string** → due date set (validated; bad format → 400 `basic_check_error`)
75: 
76: Response: `{}`
77: 
78: ### 5. `DELETE /lists/:id/:item_id` — delete item
79: No body. Response: `{}`
80: 
81: ### 6. `GET /user/:id/due-days` — calendar preview: which days have due tasks
82: The `:id` path segment is ignored — the house is taken from the JWT (same as
83: `/user/:id/overview`). Just call `GET /user/me/due-days`.
84: 
85: **Scope:** only the house's list named **`todo`** feeds the calendar. Completed
86: tasks are INCLUDED (a day stays marked after tasks are checked off).
87: 
88: Query params (both optional, combinable):
89: - `from` — `YYYY-MM-DD`; only days from this date on (inclusive, UTC)
90: - `to` — `YYYY-MM-DD`; only days before this date (**exclusive**, UTC). Invalid/absent bound → unbounded on that side.
91: 
92: Response (200), days sorted ascending, UTC dates:
93: ```json
94: {
95:   "days": [
96:     { "date": "2026-11-15", "total": 3, "open": 2 },
97:     { "date": "2026-11-20", "total": 1, "open": 0 }
98:   ]
99: }
100: ```
101: - `total` — due tasks that day (completed + open)
102: - `open` — due tasks not completed yet
103: - Empty result → `{"days": []}`
104: 
105: **Example:** month grid for November 2026: `GET /user/me/due-days?from=2026-11-01&to=2026-12-01`
106: 
107: **Calendar workflow:**
108: 1. `GET /user/me/due-days?from=<grid start>&to=<grid end + 1 day>` → mark days.
109: 2. User taps a day → find the `todo` list id from `GET /lists/` → `GET /lists/:id/?due_on=YYYY-MM-DD` to load that day's tasks.
110: 
111: ## Error codes (in `error.code`)
112: `json_format_error`, `basic_check_error`, `list_not_found`, `item_not_found`,
113: `cant_access_this` (403), `internal_error`, `not_authenticated`.
114: 
115: ## Calendar-relevant notes
116: - To toggle completion without touching text: `PATCH` with `{"completed": true}` (no `text` field).
117: - To set only a due date: `PATCH` with `{"due_at": "..."}` (no `text` field).
118: - Tasks without a due date (`due_at == null`) never match `due_on`, never appear in `due-days`, and should not appear on a calendar.
119: - All timestamps are UTC (server timezone is UTC); convert to local time in the client. Both `due_on` and `due-days` bucket by **UTC day** — for local-time correctness near midnight, filter `due_at` client-side after loading.
120: - Pagination cap is 10 items per request and there is no offset/cursor param — a day with >10 due tasks in the `todo` list cannot be fully retrieved with `due_on` alone yet (`due-days` counts are NOT capped).

4: /// Runs [request] and normalizes every failure into an [ErrorWithCode].
5: Future<T> handleDioRequest<T>(Future<T> Function() request) async {
6:   try {
7:     return await request();
8:   } on ErrorWithCode {
9:     // Already normalized (e.g. nested call): pass it through unchanged.
10:     rethrow;
11:   } on DioException catch (e) {
12:     final data = e.response?.data;
13:     if (data is Map<String, dynamic>) {
14:       throw ErrorWithCode.fromJson(data);
15:     }
16:     if (e.type == DioExceptionType.connectionTimeout ||
17:         e.type == DioExceptionType.receiveTimeout ||
18:         e.type == DioExceptionType.sendTimeout ||
19:         e.type == DioExceptionType.connectionError) {
20:       throw const ErrorWithCode(
21:         code: "network_error",
22:         message: 'Network error occurred',
23:       );
24:     }
25:     throw ErrorWithCode(
26:       code: "internal_error",
27:       message: 'An unexpected error occurred: ${e.message ?? e.type.name}',
28:     );
29:   } catch (e) {
30:     throw ErrorWithCode(
31:       code: "internal_error",
32:       message: 'An unexpected error occurred: ${e.toString()}',
33:     );
34:   }
35: }

All verified. Now I have the complete picture:

**API facts:**
- `GET /user/me/due-days?from=YYYY-MM-DD&to=YYYY-MM-DD` → `{"days": [{"date": "2026-11-15", "total": 3, "open": 2}]}` — todo list only, completed included, `to` exclusive.
- `GET /lists/:id/?due_on=YYYY-MM-DD&limit=10` → items due that UTC day.
- `PUT /lists/:id/` body `{text, due_at?}` → creates with due date.
- `PATCH /lists/:id/:item_id`:
  - text non-empty → updates text (+due_at if present); completed IGNORED.
  - text empty/absent → **ALWAYS sets completed to body value (absent = false!)** + due_at if present.
  - **Trap**: due-only PATCH must include `completed: <current>` or the task gets un-completed.
- `due_at`: RFC3339 string, or `null` to clear, absent to keep.

**Icons available:** `calendar_clock`, `calendar_x`, `chevron_left`, `chevron_right`, `clock`, `calendar_days`, `calendar_plus`, etc.

### 1. Item model with dueAt

```dart
class Item {
  final String id;
  final String text;
  final bool completed;
  final String authorId;
  final DateTime? dueAt;

  const Item({
    required this.id,
    required this.text,
    required this.completed,
    required this.authorId,
    this.dueAt,
  });

  factory Item.fromJson(Map<String, dynamic> json) {
    return Item(
      id: json['id'],
      text: json['text'],
      completed: json['completed'],
      authorId: json['author'],
      dueAt: json['due_at'] == null ? null : DateTime.parse(json['due_at']),
    );
  }

  /// [clearDueAt] wins over [dueAt] and removes the due date (copyWith can't
  /// express "set to null" with a plain nullable param).
  Item copyWith({
    String? text,
    bool? completed,
    DateTime? dueAt,
    bool clearDueAt = false,
  }) {
    return Item(
      id: id,
      text: text ?? this.text,
      completed: completed ?? this.completed,
      authorId: authorId,
      dueAt: clearDueAt ? null : (dueAt ?? this.dueAt),
    );
  }
}
```

`DateTime.parse("2026-11-15T18:00:00Z")` → UTC DateTime (isUtc true). Good. Display: `.toLocal()`.

### 2. calendar.dart model

```dart
/// One UTC day with due tasks, from `GET /user/me/due-days`.
/// See tasks_api_quickref.md §6. Only the house's `todo` list feeds it;
/// completed tasks are included in [total].
class DueDay {
  /// Date-only (no meaningful time), local-normalized via [dateOnly] at parse.
  final DateTime date;
  final int total;
  final int open;

  const DueDay({required this.date, required this.total, required this.open});

  factory DueDay.fromJson(Map<String, dynamic> json) {
    // "2026-11-15" — DateTime.parse would give local midnight; we only ever
    // compare y/m/d, so normalize with dateOnly().
    return DueDay(
      date: dateOnly(DateTime.parse(json['date'] as String)),
      total: json['total'] as int,
      open: json['open'] as int,
    );
  }
}

class DueDaysResponse {
  final List<DueDay> days;
  const DueDaysResponse({required this.days});
  factory DueDaysResponse.fromJson(Map<String, dynamic> json) => DueDaysResponse(
    days: (json['days'] as List).map((e) => DueDay.fromJson(e)).toList(),
  );
}
```

Where does `dateOnly` live? `lib/utils/`. I'll create `lib/utils/dates.dart` with:

```dart
/// Strips the time part, keeping the local calendar day. Use for any
/// day-bucket key (selected day, due-day marks) so comparisons are stable.
DateTime dateOnly(DateTime d) => DateTime(d.year, d.month, d.day);

/// "2026-11-15" — the API's YYYY-MM-DD day format (UTC-day buckets).
String formatApiDay(DateTime d) {
  final u = d.toUtc();  // hmm...
}
```

Wait, careful with the API day format. The API buckets by **UTC day**. If I send `due_on=2026-11-15`, the server matches due_at in [2026-11-15T00:00Z, 2026-11-16T00:00Z). My selected day is a LOCAL calendar day (e.g. Nov 15 local). Per my earlier decision (accept UTC-day approximation), I format the LOCAL y/m/d directly: "2026-11-15" — meaning "the UTC day Nov 15". For users near UTC this is fine; for UTC+2, a task due 23:00 local Nov 15 = 21:00 UTC Nov 15 → still matches. A task due 00:30 local Nov 15 = 22:30 UTC Nov 14 → shows on Nov 14. Edge case accepted (documented).

So `formatApiDay(DateTime d)` = local y/m/d formatted. NOT toUtc. Because the calendar day cell represents the local day, and we approximate it as the same-numbered UTC day. Consistent with due-days (which also returns UTC days that we compare by y/m/d against local grid cells).

Hmm, but there's an inconsistency risk: due-days returns UTC dates; I parse "2026-11-15" → DateTime.parse gives LOCAL midnight Nov 15 → dateOnly → local Nov 15. Grid cell for local Nov 15 → key local Nov 15. Match. And due_on fetch uses local Nov 15 formatted as "2026-11-15" → server's UTC Nov 15. Both treat the day NUMBER as the identity. Consistent approximation. 

```dart
String formatApiDay(DateTime d) =>
    '${d.year.toString().padLeft(4, '0')}-${d.month.toString().padLeft(2, '0')}-${d.day.toString().padLeft(2, '0')}';

/// "18:00" — 24h local time.
String formatTime(DateTime d) {
  final l = d.toLocal();
  return '${l.hour.toString().padLeft(2, '0')}:${l.minute.toString().padLeft(2, '0')}';
}

/// "Sat, Nov 15" — compact day label for section titles.
String formatDayLabel(DateTime d) {
  const weekdays = ['Mon', 'Tue', 'Wed', 'Thu', 'Fri', 'Sat', 'Sun'];
  const months = ['Jan', 'Feb', 'Mar', 'Apr', 'May', 'Jun', 'Jul', 'Aug', 'Sep', 'Oct', 'Nov', 'Dec'];
  return '${weekdays[d.weekday - 1]}, ${months[d.month - 1]} ${d.day}';
}

/// "November 2026" — month header.
String formatMonth(DateTime d) {
  const months = ['January', ...];
  return '${months[d.month - 1]} ${d.year}';
}
```

Also for the due row in TaskSheet: "Sat, Nov 15 · 18:00" → `'${formatDayLabel(due.toLocal())} · ${formatTime(due)}'`. Hmm, formatDayLabel takes a DateTime and uses its y/m/d — if I pass the UTC datetime without toLocal it'd show the UTC day. So always `.toLocal()` first. I'll make formatDayLabel/formatTime call toLocal() internally to be safe:

```dart
String formatTime(DateTime d) { final l = d.toLocal(); ... }
String formatDayLabel(DateTime d) { final l = d.toLocal(); ... use l.weekday, l.month, l.day }
```
Yes — safer. For dateOnly keys I keep local.

Future<ItemsResponse> getItemsFromList(String listId, {String? dueOn}) async {
  return handleDioRequest(() async {
    final res = await dio.get(
      '/lists/$listId/',
      queryParameters: {if (dueOn != null) 'due_on': dueOn},
    );

Future addItemToList(String text, String listId, {DateTime? dueAt}) async {
  return handleDioRequest(
    () => dio.put('/lists/$listId/', data: {
      'text': text,
      if (dueAt != null) 'due_at': dueAt.toUtc().toIso8601String(),
    }),
  );
}

Wait — `dueAt.toUtc().toIso8601String()` → "2026-11-15T17:00:00.000Z". Regex allows `.000`. Go time.Parse RFC3339 allows fractional. OK.

Due-only update (the trap):
```dart
/// Due-date-only PATCH (no `text`): the API's no-text branch ALWAYS writes
/// `completed` (absent field = false), so the current value must be echoed
/// back or the task silently becomes incomplete. See tasks_api_quickref §4.
Future setItemDue(
  String listId,
  String itemId, {
  required bool completed,
  DateTime? dueAt,
}) async {
  return handleDioRequest(
    () => dio.patch('/lists/$listId/$itemId', data: {
      'completed': completed,
      'due_at': dueAt?.toUtc().toIso8601String(), // null clears the due date
    }),
  );
}
```
Note: `'due_at': null` in a Dart map → JSON null → server clears. And `dueAt?.toUtc()...` gives null when dueAt==null. Elegant: one method for set & clear.

Also `editItem` — keep as-is (text only; due_at absent → preserved; completed ignored on that branch). But wait: should the sheet's edit also be able to change due? No — separate row. Keep editItem unchanged. Hmm, but what about `markItemAs` — it PATCHes {completed} with no text → no-text branch → SetItemCompleted(completed) + touchDue=false → due untouched. Correct already.

### 6. calendar_provider.dart

```dart
import 'package:flutter_riverpod/flutter_riverpod.dart';
import 'package:homies/data/models/lists.dart';
import 'package:homies/data/models/calendar.dart';
import 'package:homies/data/repos/user_repo.dart';
import 'package:homies/providers/lists_provider.dart';
import 'package:homies/providers/user_provider.dart';
import 'package:homies/utils/dates.dart';

/// The day whose tasks are shown under the month grid. Defaults to today.
class SelectedDayNotifier extends Notifier<DateTime> {
  @override
  DateTime build() => dateOnly(DateTime.now());

  void select(DateTime day) => state = dateOnly(day);
}

final selectedDayProvider = NotifierProvider<SelectedDayNotifier, DateTime>(
  SelectedDayNotifier.new,
);

/// The month shown in the grid (always the 1st, local). Follows the selected
/// day; prev/next chevrons shift it by a month.
class VisibleMonthNotifier extends Notifier<DateTime> {
  @override
  DateTime build() {
    final now = DateTime.now();
    return DateTime(now.year, now.month);
  }

  void shift(int months) {
    state = DateTime(state.year, state.month + months);
  }

  void syncTo(DateTime day) {
    final m = DateTime(day.year, day.month);
    if (m != state) state = m;
  }
}

final visibleMonthProvider = NotifierProvider<VisibleMonthNotifier, DateTime>(
  VisibleMonthNotifier.new,
);
```

Hmm — should these be autoDispose? The page is recreated on tab switch (go_router replaces the page). If NOT autoDispose, the selected day persists across tab switches (nice: user goes to todo and back, calendar still on the same day). If autoDispose, it resets to today on every visit. Which is better UX? Persisting the selection within the app session feels better. But persisting forever (across app restarts) — NotifierProvider without autoDispose lives for the app session (ProviderScope lifetime). That's fine. I'll keep them non-autoDispose (session-persistent selection), and make the DATA providers autoDispose families (so stale day data refetches on revisit).

Wait — but if selectedDay persists and calendarDayProvider(day) is autoDispose, revisiting the calendar tab refetches that day. Good.

```dart
/// Due-task marks for the visible month grid: one request per month range.
/// `to` is exclusive on the API, hence month+1.
final dueDaysProvider = FutureProvider.autoDispose
    .family<List<DueDay>, DateTime>((ref, month) {
  return ref
      .read(userRepositoryProvider)
      .getDueDays(
        from: DateTime(month.year, month.month),
        to: DateTime(month.year, month.month + 1),
      );
});
```
Note: the grid shows up to 42 cells spanning parts of adjacent months. Should I fetch a wider range (grid start..grid end) so adjacent-month days also get dots? The grid's visible range = from the Monday on/before the 1st to the Sunday on/after the last day. Fetching exactly that range gives dots on the leading/trailing days too — nicer. But then the family key should be the grid range, not the month. Simpler: key by month, fetch [month-1 day 1 ... month+2 day 1)? That over-fetches slightly but covers any grid spill (max 6 days before + 6 after). Hmm, cleanest: compute the grid range in the provider from the month anchor:

```dart
final dueDaysProvider = FutureProvider.autoDispose.family<List<DueDay>, DateTime>((ref, month) async {
  final gridStart = startOfGrid(month); // Monday on/before the 1st
  final gridEnd = DateTime(month.year, month.month + 1, 1); // exclusive-ish
  // API `to` is exclusive; add a day to include the last grid day... 
  return ref.read(userRepositoryProvider).getDueDays(from: gridStart, to: endOfGridExclusive(month));
});
```
Let me define grid helpers in dates.dart:
```dart
/// Monday on or before the 1st of [month]'s grid.
DateTime startOfGrid(DateTime month) {
  final first = DateTime(month.year, month.month);
  return first.subtract(Duration(days: first.weekday - 1)); // weekday: Mon=1
}
```
Careful with DST: subtracting Duration across a DST boundary can shift the hour. Safer: `DateTime(first.year, first.month, first.day - (first.weekday - 1))` — the DateTime constructor normalizes day<=0 into the previous month. Yes, use constructor arithmetic (DST-safe for date-only).

```dart
DateTime startOfGrid(DateTime month) {
  final first = DateTime(month.year, month.month, 1);
  return DateTime(first.year, first.month, first.day - (first.weekday - 1));
}

/// Exclusive upper bound for the grid: the day after the last cell.
DateTime endOfGridExclusive(DateTime month) {
  final last = DateTime(month.year, month.month + 1, 0); // day 0 of next month = last day of month
  return DateTime(last.year, last.month, last.day + (7 - last.weekday) + 1);
}
```
`DateTime(y, m+1, 0)` → last day of month m. Then add days to reach Sunday, +1 for exclusive. Good.

So dueDaysProvider(month) fetches the full visible grid range in ONE request. Adjacent-month days get dots too. 

Should the returned marks be a Map for O(1) lookup? The widget can build the map:
```dart
final marks = {for (final d in days) d.date: d};
```
I'll do that in the page/widget. Or provide a derived provider. Keep it in the widget build (cheap).

Calendar day tasks with optimistic mutations:

```dart
/// Tasks due on one day (todo list only), with optimistic mutations that
/// mirror TodoListNotifier. After a successful mutation the todo list and
/// the month marks are invalidated so the other views stay in sync.
class CalendarDayNotifier
    extends AutoDisposeFamilyAsyncNotifier<List<Item>, DateTime> {
  @override
  Future<List<Item>> build(DateTime day) async {
    final lists = await ref.read(listsProvider.future);
    final items = await ref
        .read(listsRepositoryProvider)
        .getItemsFromList(lists.todo.id, dueOn: formatApiDay(day));
    return sortItems(items);
  }

  /// Agenda order: by due time, undated/unparseable last (API returns
  /// created_at DESC, which is useless for a day view).
  static List<Item> sortItems(List<Item> items) {
    final sorted = [...items];
    sorted.sort((a, b) {
      final ad = a.dueAt, bd = b.dueAt;
      if (ad == null && bd == null) return 0;
      if (ad == null) return 1;
      if (bd == null) return -1;
      return ad.compareTo(bd);
    });
    return sorted;
  }

  Future<String> _todoListId() async =>
      (await ref.read(listsProvider.future)).todo.id;

  void _syncOthers() {
    ref.invalidate(todoListProvider);
    ref.invalidate(dueDaysProvider); // invalidates the whole family
  }

  Future<void> addItem(String text, DateTime dueAt) async {
    final trimmed = text.trim();
    if (trimmed.isEmpty) return;

    final current = state.value ?? [];
    final newItem = Item(
      id: 'temp_${DateTime.now().millisecondsSinceEpoch}',
      text: trimmed,
      authorId: ref.read(userProvider).valueOrNull?.uid ?? '',
      completed: false,
      dueAt: dueAt,
    );
    state = AsyncValue.data(sortItems([newItem, ...current]));

    try {
      final listId = await _todoListId();
      await ref
          .read(listsRepositoryProvider)
          .addItemToList(trimmed, listId, dueAt: dueAt);
      _syncOthers();
      ref.invalidateSelf(); // replace the temp item with server data
    } catch (_) {
      state = AsyncValue.data(current);
      rethrow;
    }
  }

  Future<void> markItemAs(bool completed, String id) async {
    await _mutate(
      id,
      (item) => item.copyWith(completed: completed),
      (listId) => ref
          .read(listsRepositoryProvider)
          .markItemAs(listId, id, completed),
    );
  }

  Future<void> editItem(String text, String id) async {
    await _mutate(
      id,
      (item) => item.copyWith(text: text),
      (listId) => ref.read(listsRepositoryProvider).editItem(text, listId, id),
    );
  }

  Future<void> deleteItem(String id) async {
    final current = state.value ?? [];
    state = AsyncValue.data(current.where((i) => i.id != id).toList());
    try {
      final listId = await _todoListId();
      await ref.read(listsRepositoryProvider).deleteItem(listId, id);
      _syncOthers();
    } catch (_) {
      state = AsyncValue.data(current);
      rethrow;
    }
  }

  /// Set ([dueAt]) or clear ([dueAt] == null) the due date. Echoes the
  /// current `completed` because of the API's no-text PATCH branch (see
  /// ListsApi.setItemDue).
  Future<void> setItemDue(String id, DateTime? dueAt) async {
    await _mutate(
      id,
      (item) => dueAt == null
          ? item.copyWith(clearDueAt: true)
          : item.copyWith(dueAt: dueAt),
      (listId) async {
        final item = (state.value ?? []).firstWhere((i) => i.id == id);
        await ref
            .read(listsRepositoryProvider)
            .setItemDue(listId, id, completed: item.completed, dueAt: dueAt);
      },
    );
  }

  Future<void> _mutate(
    String id,
    Item Function(Item) transform,
    Future<void> Function(String listId) apiCall,
  ) async {
    final current = state.value ?? [];
    state = AsyncValue.data(
      sortItems(current.map((i) => i.id == id ? transform(i) : i).toList()),
    );
    try {
      final listId = await _todoListId();
      await apiCall(listId);
      _syncOthers();
    } catch (_) {
      state = AsyncValue.data(current);
      rethrow;
    }
  }
}

final calendarDayProvider = AsyncNotifierProvider.autoDispose
    .family<CalendarDayNotifier, List<Item>, DateTime>(CalendarDayNotifier.new);
```

Wait — careful in setItemDue's apiCall: I read `state.value` AFTER the optimistic transform, so `item.completed` is the current (transformed) completed — the transform doesn't touch completed, so it's the same. Fine. But cleaner: capture completed before. Actually the transform only changes dueAt, so completed is identical. OK.

Hmm, one issue with `_mutate` + sortItems: if the due date CHANGES to another day (user reschedules from the calendar sheet to a different day), the item should disappear from this day's list. After `_syncOthers()` + the server refetch... I don't invalidateSelf in _mutate. The optimistic state keeps the item with its new dueAt (which is another day) — it would still show in this day's list until refetch. Should setItemDue invalidateSelf when the day changed? Simplest: in setItemDue, after success, if the new due date's day != this day → remove it from state (or invalidateSelf). Let me invalidateSelf() in setItemDue after success — refetches the day, server-authoritative. Slight flicker but correct. Actually invalidateSelf on an autoDispose family provider that's currently watched → refetch, state goes to AsyncLoading (with previous value retained? `ref.invalidateSelf()` triggers a rebuild → AsyncLoading with isRefresh; `.value` still holds old data during refresh). The UI uses `.when(data/loading/error)` — during refresh, `when` would show... AsyncValue.when by default has skipLoadingOnRefresh: true → shows data during refresh. Good, no flicker.

So: setItemDue → _mutate(...) then invalidateSelf()? But _mutate catches errors and rethrows; I'd add invalidateSelf after _mutate succeeds. Let me restructure setItemDue to not use _mutate, or add a `refetchAfter` flag to _mutate. Cleaner: give _mutate an optional `bool refetch = false`:

```dart
Future<void> _mutate(..., {bool refetch = false}) async {
  ...
  try {
    final listId = await _todoListId();
    await apiCall(listId);
    _syncOthers();
    if (refetch) ref.invalidateSelf();
  } catch ...
}
```
And setItemDue passes refetch: true. addItem already invalidatesSelf.

Also `_syncOthers` invalidates `dueDaysProvider` (the whole family — Riverpod 2 allows `ref.invalidate(provider)` on a family to invalidate all instances? Yes, invalidating a family provider invalidates all its instances.) Hmm, is that right in Riverpod 2.6? `ref.invalidate(dueDaysProvider)` where dueDaysProvider is a family — yes, it invalidates all family instances (documented behavior). Good.

But wait: invalidating todoListProvider on EVERY calendar mutation — the todo page isn't visible then; the provider is kept-alive (not autoDispose), so invalidation marks it stale and it refetches immediately if watched... If unwatched, does an invalidated non-autoDispose provider refetch eagerly? In Riverpod 2, invalidating a provider that has no listeners → it's disposed/recomputed lazily on next read? For non-autoDispose providers, `invalidate` marks it for recomputation; if nobody listens, recomputation happens on next read/watch. Actually Riverpod 2 keeps non-autoDispose providers alive; invalidate → the state becomes AsyncLoading and it REFETCHES eagerly? Hmm. Let me think: `ref.invalidate(provider)` on a kept-alive provider with listeners → rebuilds immediately. Without listeners... Riverpod 2.x: providers are only kept alive if listened or keepAlive. todoListProvider is a plain AsyncNotifierProvider (not autoDispose) — in Riverpod 2, non-autoDispose providers stay alive once initialized, even unlistened. Invalidate → schedules rebuild; I believe it rebuilds eagerly (since it's alive). That means an extra API fetch on each calendar mutation — acceptable (it keeps todo page fresh; and switching tabs recreates the todo page which watches it anyway).

Alternatively `ref.invalidate` only when needed... keep it simple: invalidate both. The rate limit is 10 req/s — a mutation triggers: 1 PATCH + 1 lists refetch + 1 due-days refetch ≈ 3 requests. Fine.

Hmm, also: TodoListNotifier mutations (todo page) should invalidate the calendar day + due days too, for symmetry — but the calendar page is disposed when on the todo tab, and autoDispose family data is gone; revisiting refetches. dueDaysProvider is also autoDispose family → disposed when calendar page unmounts → refetch on return. So NO changes needed in TodoListNotifier. 

One more: `selectedDayProvider` and `visibleMonthProvider` are non-autoDispose Notifiers — they persist across tab switches. Fine (session memory).

Riverpod family with DateTime arg: the arg is used as the provider key via ==/hashCode — DateTime implements value equality. dateOnly-normalized DateTimes compare equal correctly. Good.

And when a day in an adjacent month is tapped, the widget calls BOTH onDaySelected(date) and onMonthChanged(that date's month)? ```dart

Widget build(BuildContext context) {
  final gridStart = startOfGrid(month); // Monday on/before the 1st
  // 42 cells (6 weeks) always — stable height
  final cells = List.generate(42, (i) => DateTime(gridStart.year, gridStart.month, gridStart.day + i));
  
  return Container(
    width: double.infinity,
    padding: const EdgeInsets.all(8),

      color: context.colors.surfaceContainer,

          ...

}
```

Sizing: HWeekCalendar computes `boxSize = (maxWidth - totalSpacing - 16) / 7` where totalSpacing = 6*8 = 48 and the -16 accounts for the card's 8px padding each side. Wait — LayoutBuilder is INSIDE the padded container, so constraints.maxWidth already excludes the 16px padding. Yet they subtract 16 again... Let me re-read HWeekCalendar:

```dart
return LayoutBuilder(builder: (context, constraints) {
  const spacing = 8.0;
  final totalSpacing = (days.length - 1) * spacing;
  final boxSize = (constraints.maxWidth - totalSpacing - 16) / days.length;
  return Container(padding: EdgeInsets.all(8), ... Row(spaceBetween, children: boxes));
});
```
The LayoutBuilder wraps the Container, so constraints.maxWidth = full available width (card outer). The card has 8px padding inside → inner width = maxWidth - 16. Then 7 boxes with spaceBetween: totalSpacing is handled by spaceBetween, so boxSize = (innerWidth - totalSpacing)/7 = (maxWidth - 16 - 48)/7. That matches their formula. And `spaceBetween` distributes the leftover as gaps — with boxSize computed to leave exactly 48px total gap → 8px gaps. Consistent.

For the month grid I need 7 columns × 6 rows. I'll use the same approach: boxSize = (maxWidth - 16 - 48)/7, cells square, rows via Column of Rows with 8px vertical spacing... For vertical: use `Column(children: [Row(...), SizedBox(height: 8), Row(...), ...])` or a Wrap with spacing/runSpacing 8. Wrap is cleaner:
```dart
Wrap(spacing: 8, runSpacing: 8, children: cells.map(box))
```
With boxSize computed so 7 boxes + 6*8 gaps = inner width exactly. Wrap lays 7 per row.

Tap handling: InkWell with borderRadius 12 inside the cell → ripple matches. Use Material+InkWell? The card Container has a color; InkWell needs a Material ancestor — the page Scaffold provides Material. InkWell inside a Container with decoration: the ripple renders on the nearest Material (the Scaffold's), which would paint OVER the container's color? No — InkWell renders its splash inside itself if wrapped in Material; without a local Material, the splash paints on the ancestor Material which is BEHIND the container's white bg → invisible splash. Common fix: wrap in Material(color: transparent) or use GestureDetector. The task tile uses ListTile (which handles its own ink via Material in ListTile? ListTile renders ink correctly because it's Material-backed... actually ListTile inside a Container with color also has this issue; the existing HTaskTile uses ListTile directly in a decorated Container — the splash may be clipped/hidden there too, but that's existing behavior).

For the day cells, simplest and safe: `GestureDetector(onTap: ..., child: Container(decoration: ...))` — no ripple, but a clear visual selected state. Or InkWell wrapped in Material(type: MaterialType.transparency)? Hmm, that kills the bg color... The pattern: 
```dart

  borderRadius: BorderRadius.circular(12),
  child: InkWell(borderRadius: 12, onTap: ..., child: ...),
)
```
Using Material with the cell's color + InkWell gives proper ripple ON the colored cell. That's the clean Flutter way. I'll do that: each cell = Material(color: cellBg, borderRadius: 12, child: InkWell(borderRadius: 12, onTap, child: content)).

- default (in month, not selected/today): hmm — HWeekCalendar's unselected cells use `surface` bg (#F6F6F6) on the white card. For a month grid, 42 gray chips might look heavy... but consistency with HWeekCalendar says: unselected = surface bg. Let me think visually: white card, light-gray rounded cells, selected = yellow, today = yellow ring. That's cohesive with the week calendar. Yes — unselected in-month cells: `surface` bg. Outside-month cells: transparent (no chip) + dimmed text — distinguishes them nicely.

Cell content: Column(center): day number (labelMedium) + 2px + dot (4px circle) or transparent placeholder.
- Number color: selected → onPrimary; today → onSurface (with ring); outside → onSurface@0.3; else → onSecondary? HWeekCalendar unselected text uses onSecondary. Wait: `color: isSelected ? onPrimary : onSecondary`. onSecondary = #CC202020. OK: unselected in-month → onSecondary.

On primary yellow, a dark dot (onPrimary #202020) reads well. Let me do: if isSelected → dot = onPrimary; else open>0 → primary; else → onSurface@0.25. Hmm, but for open==0 on selected cell, onPrimary too. Fine: selected → onPrimary always.

Wait, but on a selected primary cell, is the dot even needed? Yes — shows the day has tasks. OK.

Cell size: boxSize square. With 6 rows: height = 6*boxSize + 5*8. On a 360dp-wide screen: card outer width = 360-48 (page padding 24) = 312. boxSize = (312 - 16 - 48)/7 = 248/7 ≈ 35.4. Height ≈ 6*35.4 + 40 = 252 + header (~40) + weekdays (~20) + paddings ≈ 340. Reasonable.

Hmm — 35px cells with a number + dot: number is labelMedium (14px) + dot 4px + 2px gap = ~20px content in 35px cell. Fits.

Weekday header row: same 7-column layout with the same boxSize (so letters align with cells): Row(spaceBetween, 7 × SizedBox(width: boxSize, child: Center(Text('M', labelMedium, onSecondary)))). Must be inside the same LayoutBuilder to share boxSize. I'll structure: LayoutBuilder → Column [header, weekdays, Wrap]. The header (month + chevrons) can be full-width (not per-cell).

Future<void> _toggle(BuildContext context, WidgetRef ref) async {

}

```dart

    borderRadius: BorderRadius.circular(16),

Hmm wait, does the shadow render correctly with a transparent Container? BoxShadow on a Container with no color → the shadow draws from the decoration's shape (rounded rect) — yes, shadows render even without a fill (the shape is used). But the shadow would be visible UNDER the Material's white bg — fine, it's offset 0,1 blur 2 → mostly hidden behind, 1px bottom edge visible. Same as the standard pattern where the Container itself is white. OK, actually simpler: put color on the Container too and use MaterialType.transparency for the ink:

```dart
Container(
  decoration: BoxDecoration(color: white, radius 16, shadow),
  child: Material(
    type: MaterialType.transparency,
    child: InkWell(borderRadius: 16, onTap, child: Padding(Row)),
  ),
)
```
Hmm — MaterialType.transparency inside a colored Container: the InkWell splash renders on the transparent Material, which sits above the Container's color. That works (splash visible over white). But the ripple's rectangular bounds get clipped by borderRadius. Yes, this is a known working pattern. I'll use it.

Pickers flow (`_pickDueDateTime`):
```dart
Future<void> _pickDueDateTime() async {
  final now = DateTime.now();
  final current = _dueAt?.toLocal();
  final date = await showDatePicker(
    context: context,
    initialDate: current ?? now,
    firstDate: DateTime(now.year - 1),
    lastDate: DateTime(now.year + 5),
  );
  if (date == null || !mounted) return;
  final time = await showTimePicker(
    context: context,
    initialTime: TimeOfDay.fromDateTime(current ?? DateTime(now.year, now.month, now.day, 12)),
  );
  if (time == null || !mounted) return;
  await _saveDue(DateTime(date.year, date.month, date.day, time.hour, time.minute));
}
```
Default hour when no existing due: 12:00 (noon) — reasonable. For the calendar new-task flow: default 12:00 too? Or the next full hour? Noon is fine and predictable. Hmm, for a new task on a specific day, maybe default to 9:00 (morning)? I'll use 12:00 consistently... Actually for NEW tasks, defaulting to the next hour from now (if the selected day is today) is smarter. Keep it simple: 12:00 default everywhere. Fine.

`_saveDue`:
```dart
Future<void> _saveDue(DateTime? due) async {
  final previous = _dueAt;
  setState(() => _dueAt = due);
  try {
    final hook = widget.onDueChanged;
    if (hook != null) {
      await hook(due);
    } else {
      await ref.read(todoListProvider.notifier).setItemDue(widget.id, due, completed: _completed);
    }
  } catch (e) {
    if (!mounted) return;
    setState(() => _dueAt = previous);
    showTaskError(context, e);
  }
}
```
Wait — the default (todo page) path needs a `setItemDue` on TodoListNotifier too! Since TaskSheet is shared, the todo page's sheet will also show the due row (dueAt null → "No due date", tap → set one). That's GOOD: the user asked for due-date editing in TaskSheet (which is used by the todo page). So TodoListNotifier needs `setItemDue(id, dueAt, {completed})` with optimistic update + revert. Yes — add it to TodoListNotifier mirroring the pattern:

```dart
/// Set or clear ([dueAt] == null) the due date. Echoes [completed] because
/// the API's no-text PATCH branch always writes `completed` (absent = false).
Future<void> setItemDue(String id, DateTime? dueAt, {required bool completed}) async {
  await _mutate(
    id,
    (item) => dueAt == null ? item.copyWith(clearDueAt: true) : item.copyWith(dueAt: dueAt),
    (listId) => ref.read(listsRepositoryProvider).setItemDue(listId, id, completed: completed, dueAt: dueAt),
  );
}
```
Hmm — but TodoListNotifier._mutate doesn't re-sort (todo order = created_at DESC). Setting a due date shouldn't reorder the todo list. Fine, no sort there.

Also: after a todo-page due change, should the calendar refresh? The calendar page is disposed while on todo (autoDispose data). On return it refetches. 

And onDueChanged callback signature: `Future<void> Function(DateTime? dueAt)`. The calendar page binds id+completed... wait, `completed` for the API echo: the calendar notifier's setItemDue reads current completed from its own state (as designed). So the callback only needs dueAt. But careful: TaskSheet's `_completed` may differ from the provider state if toggled in-sheet... The sheet's toggle updates the provider optimistically too (same state), so they stay in sync. In the calendar notifier's setItemDue I read completed from `state.value` — which reflects the sheet's toggle (since toggle → provider.markItemAs → optimistic state update). Good.

Hmm, wait — there's an ordering subtlety in TaskSheet: `_toggle()` updates `_completed` (local) AND calls the provider. If the provider call fails, both revert. Consistent.

```dart
class CalendarPage extends ConsumerWidget {
  const CalendarPage({super.key});

  @override
  Widget build(BuildContext context, WidgetRef ref) {
    final selectedDay = ref.watch(selectedDayProvider);
    final visibleMonth = ref.watch(visibleMonthProvider);
    final dueDaysAsync = ref.watch(dueDaysProvider(visibleMonth));
    final dayTasksAsync = ref.watch(calendarDayProvider(selectedDay));
    final user = ref.watch(userProvider).valueOrNull;
    final home = ref.watch(homeProvider).valueOrNull;
    final isOnline = ref.watch(connectivityProvider);

    final dueDaysError = dueDaysAsync.isLoading ? null : dueDaysAsync.error;
    final dayError = dayTasksAsync.isLoading ? null : dayTasksAsync.error;
    final error = dayError ?? dueDaysError;
    final showCard = !isOnline || error != null;
    final cardMessage = !isOnline
        ? "You're offline — some data may be unavailable"
        : error is ErrorWithCode
        ? error.message
        : 'Something went wrong';

    final markedDays = {
      for (final d in dueDaysAsync.valueOrNull ?? const <DueDay>[]) d.date: d,
    };

    return GestureDetector(
      onTap: () => FocusScope.of(context).unfocus(),
      child: Scaffold(
        appBar: AppBar(brand...),
        backgroundColor: context.colors.surface,
        bottomNavigationBar: const HNavBar(currentIndex: 2),
        body: Column(children: [
          if (showCard) Padding(fromLTRB(24,24,24,0), HOfflineCard(message, icon, onRetry: () { ref.invalidate(dueDaysProvider); ref.invalidate(calendarDayProvider(selectedDay)); })),
          Expanded(
            child: RefreshIndicator(
              onRefresh: () async {
                await Future.wait([
                  ref.refresh(dueDaysProvider(visibleMonth).future),
                  ref.refresh(calendarDayProvider(selectedDay).future),
                ]);
              },
              child: ListView(
                physics: const AlwaysScrollableScrollPhysics(),
                padding: const EdgeInsets.all(24),
                children: [
                  const SizedBox(height: 24)?,  // todo page has 24 top gap; home too. Keep.
                  HMonthCalendar(
                    month: visibleMonth,
                    selected: selectedDay,
                    markedDays: markedDays,
                    onDaySelected: (day) {
                      ref.read(selectedDayProvider.notifier).select(day);
                      ref.read(visibleMonthProvider.notifier).syncTo(day);
                    },
                    onMonthChanged: (m) => ref.read(visibleMonthProvider.notifier).setMonth(m),
                  ),
                  const SizedBox(height: 32),
                  Text(sectionTitle, style: headlineMedium),
                  const SizedBox(height: 12),
                  IgnorePointer(ignoring: !dayTasksAsync.hasValue, child: Opacity(opacity: hasValue ? 1 : 0.5, child: NewCalendarTaskTile(day: selectedDay))),
                  const SizedBox(height: 12),
                  ...dayTasksAsync.when(
                    data: (items) => items.isEmpty ? [empty state] : [for each: Padding(bottom 12, HTaskTile(...))],
                    loading: () => [SizedBox(height: 150, child: Center(CircularProgressIndicator))],
                    error: (_, __) => [SizedBox(height: 100)],  // card reports
                  ),
                  const SizedBox(height: 24),
                ],
              ),
            ),
          ),
        ]),
      ),
    );
  }
}

class _NewCalendarTaskTileState extends ConsumerState<NewCalendarTaskTile> {
  final TextEditingController _controller = TextEditingController();
  bool _submitting = false; // guard double taps / picker reopen

  @override
  void dispose() { _controller.dispose(); super.dispose(); }

  Future<void> _submit() async {
    final text = _controller.text.trim();
    if (text.isEmpty || _submitting) return;
    setState(() => _submitting = true);
    try {
      // The day is known; only the hour is missing — ask for it.
      final time = await showTimePicker(
        context: context,
        initialTime: const TimeOfDay(hour: 12, minute: 0),
        helpText: 'Due at',   // hmm, helpText customization
      );
      if (time == null || !mounted) return;   // cancelled → keep the text
      final dueAt = DateTime(widget.day.year, widget.day.month, widget.day.day, time.hour, time.minute);
      await ref.read(calendarDayProvider(widget.day).notifier).addItem(text, dueAt);
      _controller.clear();
    } catch (e) {
      if (mounted) showTaskError(context, e);
    } finally {
      if (mounted) setState(() => _submitting = false);
    }
  }
  ...
}
```

### 12. DueDay map key normalization
`DueDay.fromJson` uses `dateOnly(DateTime.parse("2026-11-15"))` → DateTime.parse of a date-only string gives LOCAL midnight → dateOnly → local (y,m,d). Grid cells are local DateTimes from DateTime(y,m,d) arithmetic → equal keys. ✓

dates.dart content:

```dart
/// Date & time formatting helpers for the calendar.
///
/// The API buckets days by **UTC** date (`due_on`, `/user/me/due-days`), while
/// the UI works with local calendar days. We accept the UTC-day approximation:
/// a local day number is used as the UTC day number (see tasks_api_quickref.md
/// §"Calendar-relevant notes"). All day keys in the app are normalized with
/// [dateOnly] so map lookups and comparisons are stable.

/// Strips the time part, keeping the local calendar day.
DateTime dateOnly(DateTime d) => DateTime(d.year, d.month, d.day);

/// Monday on or before the 1st of [month] — the first cell of a month grid.
DateTime startOfGrid(DateTime month) {
  final first = DateTime(month.year, month.month, 1);
  return DateTime(first.year, first.month, first.day - (first.weekday - 1));
}

/// The day after the last cell of [month]'s grid (Sunday on or after the last
/// day, +1) — exclusive upper bound, matching the due-days API's `to`.
DateTime endOfGridExclusive(DateTime month) {
  final last = DateTime(month.year, month.month + 1, 0);
  return DateTime(last.year, last.month, last.day + (7 - last.weekday) + 1);
}

/// "2026-11-15" — the API's YYYY-MM-DD day format. Uses the LOCAL day number
/// (UTC-day approximation, see file header).
String formatApiDay(DateTime d) {
  final y = d.year.toString().padLeft(4, '0');
  final m = d.month.toString().padLeft(2, '0');
  final day = d.day.toString().padLeft(2, '0');
  return '$y-$m-$day';
}

/// "18:00" — 24h local time.
String formatTime(DateTime d) {
  final l = d.toLocal();
  final h = l.hour.toString().padLeft(2, '0');
  final m = l.minute.toString().padLeft(2, '0');
  return '$h:$m';
}

const _weekdays = ['Mon', 'Tue', 'Wed', 'Thu', 'Fri', 'Sat', 'Sun'];
const _monthsShort = ['Jan', 'Feb', 'Mar', 'Apr', 'May', 'Jun', 'Jul', 'Aug', 'Sep', 'Oct', 'Nov', 'Dec'];
const _monthsLong = ['January', 'February', 'March', 'April', 'May', 'June', 'July', 'August', 'September', 'October', 'November', 'December'];

/// "Sat, Nov 15" (local).
String formatDayLabel(DateTime d) {
  final l = d.toLocal();
  return '${_weekdays[l.weekday - 1]}, ${_monthsShort[l.month - 1]} ${l.day}';
}

/// "November 2026".
String formatMonth(DateTime d) => '${_monthsLong[d.month - 1]} ${d.year}';

/// Due-date line for a task tile: time-only when due today, day + time
/// otherwise ("Due at 18:00" / "Due Sat, Nov 15 at 18:00").
String formatDueLabel(DateTime due) {
  final l = due.toLocal();
  if (dateOnly(l) == dateOnly(DateTime.now())) {
    return 'Due at ${formatTime(l)}';
  }
  return 'Due ${formatDayLabel(l)} at ${formatTime(l)}';
}
```

Note: single quotes in strings — the codebase uses double quotes mostly but both appear. Dart strings with apostrophes ("Today's Tasks") need double quotes or escaping. I'll use the codebase's mixed style carefully.

calendar.dart model:

```dart
import 'package:homies/utils/dates.dart';

/// One UTC day with due tasks, from `GET /user/me/due-days` (todo list only,
/// completed tasks included in [total]). See tasks_api_quickref.md §6.
class DueDay {
  /// Date-only key, normalized with [dateOnly] so it matches grid cells.
  final DateTime date;
  final int total;
  final int open;

  const DueDay({required this.date, required this.total, required this.open});

  factory DueDay.fromJson(Map<String, dynamic> json) {
    return DueDay(
      date: dateOnly(DateTime.parse(json['date'] as String)),
      total: json['total'] as int,
      open: json['open'] as int,
    );
  }
}

class DueDaysResponse {
  final List<DueDay> days;

  const DueDaysResponse({required this.days});

  factory DueDaysResponse.fromJson(Map<String, dynamic> json) {
    return DueDaysResponse(
      days: (json['days'] as List).map((e) => DueDay.fromJson(e)).toList(),
    );
  }
}
```

```dart
class Item {
  final String id;
  final String text;
  final bool completed;
  final String authorId;

  const Item({
    required this.id,
    required this.text,
    required this.completed,
    required this.authorId,
  });

  factory Item.fromJson(Map<String, dynamic> json) {
    return Item(
      id: json['id'],
      text: json['text'],
      completed: json['completed'],
      authorId: json['author'],
    );
  }

  Item copyWith({String? text, bool? completed}) {
    return Item(
      id: id,
      text: text ?? this.text,
      completed: completed ?? this.completed,
      authorId: authorId,
    );
  }
}

So a due-date-only PATCH `{due_at: ...}` without `completed` would silently **un-complete** the task. The quickref's "set only a due date" tip is a trap; I'll always echo the current `completed` value.
2. Text-only edits do **not** wipe `due_at` (verified `UpdateItemEx`).
3. The `due_at` regex accepts Dart's `toIso8601String()` (fractional seconds OK).
4. Icons `calendar_clock`, `calendar_x`, `chevron_left/right`, `sun` all exist in flutter_lucide 1.8.2; `AutoDisposeFamilyAsyncNotifier` exists in Riverpod 2.

{
  "content": "/// Date & time formatting helpers for the calendar.\n///\n/// The API buckets days by **UTC** date (`due_on`, `/user/me/due-days`), while\n/// the UI works with local calendar days. We accept the UTC-day approximation:\n/// a local day number is sent/compared as the same-numbered UTC day (see\n/// tasks_api_quickref.md, \"Calendar-relevant notes\"). All day keys in the app\n/// are normalized with [dateOnly] so map lookups and comparisons are stable.\n\n/// Strips the time part, keeping the local calendar day.\nDateTime dateOnly(DateTime d) => DateTime(d.year, d.month, d.day);\n\n/// Monday on or before the 1st of [month]'s grid — its first cell.\nDateTime startOfGrid(DateTime month) {\n  final first = DateTime(month.year, month.month, 1);\n  return DateTime(first.year, first.month, first.day - (first.weekday - 1));\n}\n\n/// The day after the last cell of [month]'s grid (Sunday on or after the last\n/// day, plus one) — exclusive upper bound, matching the due-days API's `to`.\nDateTime endOfGridExclusive(DateTime month) {\n  final last = DateTime(month.year, month.month + 1, 0);\n  return DateTime(last.year, last.month, last.day + (7 - last.weekday) + 1);\n}\n\n/// \"2026-11-15\" — the API's YYYY-MM-DD day format. Uses the LOCAL day number\n/// (UTC-day approximation, see file header).\nString formatApiDay(DateTime d) {\n  final y = d.year.toString().padLeft(4, '0');\n  final m = d.month.toString().padLeft(2, '0');\n  final day = d.day.toString().padLeft(2, '0');\n  return '$y-$m-$day';\n}\n\n/// \"18:00\" — 24h local time.\nString formatTime(DateTime d) {\n  final l = d.toLocal();\n  final h = l.hour.toString().padLeft(2, '0');\n  final m = l.minute.toString().padLeft(2, '0');\n  return '$h:$m';\n}\n\nconst _weekdays = [\n  'Mon',\n  'Tue',\n  'Wed',\n  'Thu',\n  'Fri',\n  'Sat',\n  'Sun',\n];\n\nconst _monthsShort = [\n  'Jan',\n  'Feb',\n  'Mar',\n  'Apr',\n  'May',\n  'Jun',\n  'Jul',\n  'Aug',\n  'Sep',\n  'Oct',\n  'Nov',\n  'Dec',\n];\n\nconst _monthsLong = [\n  'January',\n  'February',\n  'March',\n  'April',\n  'May',\n  'June',\n  'July',\n  'August',\n  'September',\n  'October',\n  'November',\n  'December',\n];\n\n/// \"Sat, Nov 15\" (local).\nString formatDayLabel(DateTime d) {\n  final l = d.toLocal();\n  return '${_weekdays[l.weekday - 1]}, ${_monthsShort[l.month - 1]} ${l.day}';\n}\n\n/// \"November 2026\" — month grid header.\nString formatMonth(DateTime d) => '${_monthsLong[d.month - 1]} ${d.year}';\n\n/// Due-date line for a task tile: time-only when due today, day + time\n/// otherwise (\"Due at 18:00\" / \"Due Sat, Nov 15 at 18:00\").\nString formatDueLabel(DateTime due) {\n  final l = due.toLocal();\n  if (dateOnly(l) == dateOnly(DateTime.now())) {\n    return 'Due at ${formatTime(l)}';\n  }\n  return 'Due ${formatDayLabel(l)} at ${formatTime(l)}';\n}\n",

Defaults to today and\n/// survives tab switches (session-scoped, not autoDispose).\nclass SelectedDayNotifier extends Notifier<DateTime> {\n  @override\n  DateTime build() => dateOnly(DateTime.now());\n\n  void select(DateTime day) => state = dateOnly(day);\n}\n\nfinal selectedDayProvider = NotifierProvider<SelectedDayNotifier, DateTime>(\n  SelectedDayNotifier.new,\n);\n\n/// The month shown in the grid (always the 1st, local). Follows the selected\n/// day; the month chevrons shift it. Session-scoped like [selectedDayProvider].\nclass VisibleMonthNotifier extends Notifier<DateTime> {\n  @override\n  DateTime build() {\n    final now = DateTime.now();\n    return DateTime(now.year, now.month);\n  }\n\n  void setMonth(DateTime month) => state = DateTime(month.year, month.month);\n\n  /// Keeps the grid on the month of [day] (tapping a day of an adjacent\n  /// month, coming back from another tab, ...).\n  void syncTo(DateTime day) {\n    final month = DateTime(day.year, day.month);\n    if (month != state) {\n      state = month;\n    }\n  }\n}\n\nfinal visibleMonthProvider = NotifierProvider<VisibleMonthNotifier, DateTime>(\n  VisibleMonthNotifier.new,\n);\n\n/// Due-task marks for the visible month grid, in ONE request covering the\n/// whole grid range (leading/trailing days of adjacent months included).\n/// The API's `to` bound is exclusive — [endOfGridExclusive] already accounts\n/// for it. Invalidating the family (no arg) refreshes every live instance.\nfinal dueDaysProvider = FutureProvider.autoDispose\n    .family<List<DueDay>, DateTime>((ref, month) {\n      return ref\n          .read(userRepositoryProvider)\n          .getDueDays(\n            from: startOfGrid(month),\n            to: endOfGridExclusive(month),\n          );\n    });\n\n/// Tasks due on one day (house's `todo` list only), with optimistic mutations\n/// mirroring [TodoListNotifier]. After a successful mutation the todo list and\n/// the month marks are invalidated so every view stays in sync; the other way\n/// around needs nothing — this family is autoDispose, so returning to the\n/// calendar page refetches anyway.\nclass CalendarDayNotifier\n    extends AutoDisposeFamilyAsyncNotifier<List<Item>, DateTime> {\n  @override\n  Future<List<Item>> build(DateTime day) async {\n    final lists = await ref.read(listsProvider.future);\n\n    final items = await ref\n        .read(listsRepositoryProvider)\n        .getItemsFromList(lists.todo.id, dueOn: formatApiDay(day));\n\n    return _byDueTime(items);\n  }\n\n  /// Agenda order: by due time ascending. (The API returns created_at DESC,\n  /// which is useless for a day view.) Items without a due date can't appear\n  /// here — `due_on` excludes them — but sort defensively anyway.\n  static List<Item> _byDueTime(List<Item> items) {\n    final sorted = [...items];\n    sorted.sort((a, b) {\n      final ad = a.dueAt;\n      final bd = b.dueAt;\n      if (ad == null && bd == null) return 0;\n      if (ad == null) return 1;\n      if (bd == null) return -1;\n      return ad.compareTo(bd);\n    });\n    return sorted;\n  }\n\n  Future<String> _todoListId() async =>\n      (await ref.read(listsProvider.future)).todo.id;\n\n  void _syncOtherViews() {\n    ref.invalidate(todoListProvider);\n    ref.invalidate(dueDaysProvider);\n  }\n\n  /// Creates a task due on this provider's day at the time carried by\n  /// [dueAt]. Optimistic insert with a temp id, then refetch (the API only\n  /// returns the new id).\n  Future<void> addItem(String text, DateTime dueAt) async {\n    final trimmed = text.trim();\n    if (trimmed.isEmpty) return;\n\n    final current = state.value ?? [];\n\n    final newItem = Item(\n      id: 'temp_${DateTime.now().millisecondsSinceEpoch}',\n      text: trimmed,\n      authorId: ref.read(userProvider).valueOrNull?.uid ?? '',\n      completed: false,\n      dueAt: dueAt,\n    );\n\n    state = AsyncValue.data(_byDueTime([newItem, ...current]));\n\n    try {\n      final listId = await _todoListId();\n      await ref\n          .read(listsRepositoryProvider)\n          .addItemToList(trimmed, listId, dueAt: dueAt);\n      _syncOtherViews();\n      // Replace the temp item with the real server-side items.\n      ref.invalidateSelf();\n    } catch (_) {\n      state = AsyncValue.data(current);\n      rethrow;\n    }\n  }\n\n  Future<void> markItemAs(bool completed, String id) async {\n    await _mutate(\n      id,\n      (item) => item.copyWith(completed: completed),\n      (listId) => ref\n          .read(listsRepositoryProvider)\n          .markItemAs(listId, id, completed),\n    );\n  }\n\n  Future<void> editItem(String text, String id) async {\n    await _mutate(\n      id,\n      (item) => item.copyWith(text: text),\n      (listId) => ref.read(listsRepositoryProvider).editItem(text, listId, id),\n    );\n  }\n\n  Future<void> deleteItem(String id) async {\n    final current = state.value ?? Design decisions recap for HMonthCalendar:
- White card, radius 16, soft shadow, padding 8 (matching HWeekCalendar)
- Header: [chevron chip 40×40 secondary] [month name titleMedium centered] [chevron chip]
- Weekday row: M T W T F S S (labelMedium, onSecondary), same cell widths
- 6×7 grid via Wrap(spacing: 8, runSpacing: 8), cell size = (maxWidth - 16 - 48) / 7
- Cell states: selected (primary fill, onPrimary text), today (2px primary border ring via Material shape), outside month (transparent, dimmed text onSurface@0.3), default (surface fill, onSecondary text)
- Dot marker: 4px circle, 2px below the number; open>0 → primary (onPrimary when selected), all completed → onSurface@0.25; placeholder SizedBox to keep alignment
- Cell = Material(color, shape/borderRadius) > InkWell(borderRadius 12) > content

Wait — for today's ring: Material(shape: RoundedRectangleBorder(side: BorderSide(color: primary, width: 2), borderRadius: BorderRadius.circular(12)), color: ...). If today is also selected → primary fill, ring irrelevant (same color). If today is outside month? Can't be (today is always in its own month grid... well, today could be in the leading/trailing cells only if the grid shows a different month — e.g. viewing December while today is Nov 30? No: if viewing December, the grid's leading cells are late November — today (Nov 30) could appear as an outside cell in the December grid! Then it'd get the today ring + dimmed text. Hmm — ring + dimmed text is contradictory. Let me prioritize: outside-month cells never show the today ring (just dimmed). Actually, showing today's ring even in the adjacent-month spill is arguably helpful... but dimmed text + ring is odd. Decision: today ring only when inMonth. Simple.

Also dots on outside-month days: the due-days fetch covers the whole grid range, so adjacent-month days CAN have marks. Show dots on them too (dimmed? no — dots use their own colors; a primary dot on a dimmed day is fine and informative). I'll show marks regardless of inMonth.

Cell content layout: Column(mainAxisSize: min, mainAxisAlignment: center, children: [Text(day), SizedBox(2), dot-or-placeholder]). Centered in the square cell via Container alignment or the Column's main axis. Since the cell is square and the content is ~20px tall, center vertically: use `Center(child: Column(mainAxisSize: MainAxisSize.min, ...))` or Column with mainAxisAlignment center + mainAxisSize max. Simpler: SizedBox(width: boxSize, height: boxSize, child: Column(mainAxisAlignment: MainAxisAlignment.center, children: [...])).

InkWell child needs to fill the cell for the tap target: Material > InkWell > SizedBox(boxSize). Structure:

```dart
Widget _dayCell(BuildContext context, DateTime day, double size, DateTime today) {
  final inMonth = day.month == month.month;
  final isSelected = day == selected;
  final isToday = inMonth && day == today;
  final mark = markedDays[day];

  Color bg;
  Color textColor;
  if (isSelected) {
    bg = context.colors.primary;
    textColor = context.colors.onPrimary;
  } else if (isToday) {
    bg = context.colors.surface;
    textColor = context.colors.onSurface;
  } else if (inMonth) {
    bg = context.colors.surface;
    textColor = context.colors.onSecondary;
  } else {
    bg = Colors.transparent;
    textColor = context.colors.onSurface.withValues(alpha: 0.3);
  }

  final dotColor = mark == null
      ? null
      : isSelected
      ? context.colors.onPrimary
      : mark.open > 0
      ? context.colors.primary
      : context.colors.onSurface.withValues(alpha: 0.25);

  return SizedBox(
    width: size,
    height: size,
    child: Material(
      color: bg,
      shape: RoundedRectangleBorder(
        borderRadius: BorderRadius.circular(12),
        side: isToday && !isSelected
            ? BorderSide(color: context.colors.primary, width: 2)
            : BorderSide.none,
      ),
      child: InkWell(
        borderRadius: BorderRadius.circular(12),
        onTap: () => onDaySelected(day),
        child: Column(
          mainAxisAlignment: MainAxisAlignment.center,
          children: [
            Text('${day.day}', style: context.texts.labelMedium!.copyWith(color: textColor)),
            const SizedBox(height: 2),
            Container(
              width: 4,
              height: 4,
              decoration: BoxDecoration(
                shape: BoxShape.circle,
                color: dotColor ?? Colors.transparent,
              ),
            ),
          ],
        ),
      ),
    ),
  );
}
```

Hmm — Material with `shape` + `color`: when using shape, Material clips to the shape. Good.

Widget signature:
```dart
class HMonthCalendar extends StatelessWidget {
  const HMonthCalendar({
    super.key,
    required this.month,
    required this.selected,
    required this.onDaySelected,
    required this.onMonthChanged,
    this.markedDays = const {},
  });

  /// Any day within the shown month (the grid derives its own bounds).
  final DateTime month;
  /// Date-only (see dateOnly()).
  final DateTime selected;
  /// Fires for every tapped day, including leading/trailing days of adjacent
  /// months — the page decides whether to follow with a month change.
  final ValueChanged<DateTime> onDaySelected;
  final ValueChanged<DateTime> onMonthChanged;
  /// Due-task marks keyed by date-only day (see DueDay).
  final Map<DateTime, DueDay> markedDays;
}
```

Full build:
```dart
@override
Widget build(BuildContext context) {
  final today = dateOnly(DateTime.now());
  final gridStart = startOfGrid(month);
  final cells = List.generate(
    42,
    (i) => DateTime(gridStart.year, gridStart.month, gridStart.day + i),
  );

  return Container(
    width: double.infinity,
    padding: const EdgeInsets.all(8),
    decoration: BoxDecoration(
      color: context.colors.surfaceContainer,
      borderRadius: BorderRadius.circular(16),
      boxShadow: [
        BoxShadow(
          color: context.colors.onSurface.withValues(alpha: 0.25),
          blurRadius: 2,
          offset: const Offset(0, 1),
        ),
      ],
    ),
    child: Column(
      mainAxisSize: MainAxisSize.min,
      children: [
        _header(context),
        const SizedBox(height: 8),
        LayoutBuilder(
          builder: (context, constraints) {
            // Same cell math as HWeekCalendar: available width minus the
            // card's 8px paddings and six 8px gaps, split across 7 columns.
            const spacing = 8.0;
            final boxSize =
                (constraints.maxWidth - 16 - (7 - 1) * spacing) / 7;

            return Column(
              mainAxisSize: MainAxisSize.min,
              children: [
                Row(
                  mainAxisAlignment: MainAxisAlignment.spaceBetween,
                  children: List.generate(7, (i) => SizedBox(
                    width: boxSize,
                    child: Text(
                      _weekdayLabels[i],
                      textAlign: TextAlign.center,
                      style: context.texts.labelMedium!.copyWith(
                        color: context.colors.onSecondary,
                      ),
                    ),
                  )),
                ),
                const SizedBox(height: 8),
                Wrap(
                  spacing: spacing,
                  runSpacing: spacing,
                  children: [
                    for (final day in cells)
                      _dayCell(context, day, boxSize, today),
                  ],
                ),
              ],
            );
          },
        ),
      ],
    ),
  );
}
```

Wait — Wrap with spacing: Wrap's spacing applies between children horizontally; with exactly 7 children per row and width = 7*boxSize + 6*8 = constraints.maxWidth - 16, the Wrap width must equal that. Wrap sizes itself to max width available (inside the padded container: constraints.maxWidth - 16 ✓ since LayoutBuilder is inside the padding... hold on. LayoutBuilder is INSIDE the Container's padding? The Container has padding 8 → its child (Column) gets maxWidth = containerWidth - 16. LayoutBuilder is inside that Column → constraints.maxWidth = containerWidth - 16. So boxSize = (constraints.maxWidth - 16 - 48)/7 would DOUBLE-subtract the padding!

Let me re-check HWeekCalendar: there, LayoutBuilder is the OUTER widget (returned directly), wrapping the Container. So constraints.maxWidth = full available width, and the Container's padding 8 is inside → inner width = maxWidth - 16. Their formula: (maxWidth - totalSpacing - 16)/7 ✓.

In my structure, the LayoutBuilder sits inside the padded Column, so constraints.maxWidth is ALREADY the inner width. Then boxSize = (constraints.maxWidth - 48)/7 (only gaps). And the Row/Wrap width = 7*boxSize + 48 = constraints.maxWidth ✓.

Let me restructure to avoid confusion — put the header inside, and compute: `final boxSize = (constraints.maxWidth - (7 - 1) * spacing) / 7;` since maxWidth here is post-padding. ✓ 

But floating point: 7*boxSize + 6*8 might exceed maxWidth by an epsilon → Wrap pushes the 7th cell to the next row! Dangerous. Mitigate: subtract a tiny fudge, or use floor. HWeekCalendar uses Row+spaceBetween which can't overflow. For Wrap, overflow wraps → broken grid. Safer: build rows manually with Row(spaceBetween) per week, like HWeekCalendar does for one row:

```dart
Column(children: [
  for (var w = 0; w < 6; w++) ...[
    if (w > 0) const SizedBox(height: 8),
    Row(mainAxisAlignment: MainAxisAlignment.spaceBetween, children: [
      for (var d = 0; d < 7; d++) _dayCell(context, cells[w*7+d], boxSize, today),
    ]),
  ],
])
```
With spaceBetween and boxSize slightly under the true share, gaps absorb the slack — no overflow risk. boxSize = (maxWidth - 48) / 7 could still be a repeating decimal; Row with spaceBetween: total children width = 7*boxSize ≤ maxWidth - 48 + ε... if 7*boxSize > maxWidth - 48*... hmm, spaceBetween distributes (maxWidth - 7*boxSize) as 6 gaps; if that's negative → overflow error. boxSize = (maxWidth - 48)/7 → 7*boxSize = maxWidth - 48 exactly (modulo float ε). Float ε could make it maxWidth - 48 + 1e-13 → gaps total 48 - 1e-13 → still positive. Fine. But to be extra safe I can floor to whole pixels: `boxSize = ((maxWidth - 48) / 7).floorToDouble()`. Slight leftover absorbed by spaceBetween. Clean. I'll floor it.

Same for the weekday header row (spaceBetween with boxSize widths). ✓

Vertical rhythm: 6 rows with 8px runSpacing → same as Wrap would give.

Now — the cells: 42 always (6 weeks). Some months need only 5 rows, but fixed 6 keeps the card height stable across month changes (no layout jump). Good choice.

One more consideration: tapping a leading/trailing day (adjacent month) — onDaySelected fires; the page also syncs the month. ✓ (decided earlier).

Row structure:
```dart
Container(
  decoration: BoxDecoration(borderRadius: 16, boxShadow: [soft]),
  child: Material(
    type: MaterialType.transparency,
    child: InkWell(
      borderRadius: 16,

        ...

```
Careful: MaterialType.transparency + child Container(color) → the Container paints OVER the material's ink → splash invisible. The correct pattern is Material(color: white, borderRadius) > InkWell > content WITHOUT its own bg. So:

```dart
Container(
  decoration: BoxDecoration(
    borderRadius: BorderRadius.circular(16),
    boxShadow: [soft shadow],
  ),
  child: Material(
    color: context.colors.surfaceContainer,
    borderRadius: BorderRadius.circular(16),
    child: InkWell(
      borderRadius: BorderRadius.circular(16),
      onTap: _pickDue,
      child: Padding(
        padding: const EdgeInsets.fromLTRB(16, 12, 8, 12),
        child: Row(...),
      ),
    ),
  ),
)
```
Shadow on the outer transparent Container + white Material inside. The shadow renders behind the Material — visible around edges (offset 0,1 → bottom edge). Works. (Same trick as HTaskTile's outer Container decoration with the ListTile inside — wait, HTaskTile puts color AND shadow on the same Container and the ListTile's ink... ListTile creates its own Material? No. Hmm, existing tile: Container(color: white, shadow) > ListTile(onTap) — ListTile's splash paints on the nearest Material ancestor ABOVE the Container... which is the Scaffold's Material — behind the white container → splash hidden?! Actually no: InkResponse from ListTile paints into the Material's ink layer; the Material is the Scaffold body's Material, which is BELOW the Container in paint order → the white Container paints over the splash. So the existing task tile has an invisible ripple (or clipped). Whatever — existing behavior, not my problem. For my new due row I'll use the Material-first pattern so the ripple works.)

Now the picker flow. showDatePicker params: context, initialDate, firstDate, lastDate. Bounds: firstDate = now - 1 year, lastDate = now + 5 years. initialDate = existing due (local) or today. After date → showTimePicker(initialTime: from existing due or 12:00). Then combine into local DateTime → _saveDue.

```dart
Future<void> _pickDue() async {
  final now = DateTime.now();
  final current = _dueAt?.toLocal();

  final date = await showDatePicker(
    context: context,
    initialDate: current ?? dateOnly(now),
    firstDate: DateTime(now.year - 1),
    lastDate: DateTime(now.year + 5),
  );
  if (date == null || !mounted) return;

  final time = await showTimePicker(
    context: context,
    initialTime: TimeOfDay.fromDateTime(
      current ?? DateTime(now.year, now.month, now.day, 12),
    ),
  );
  if (time == null || !mounted) return;

  await _saveDue(
    DateTime(date.year, date.month, date.day, time.hour, time.minute),
  );
}

Future<void> _removeDue() => _saveDue(null);

Future<void> _saveDue(DateTime? due) async {
  final previous = _dueAt;
  setState(() => _dueAt = due);
  try {
    final hook = widget.onDueChanged;
    if (hook != null) {
      await hook(due);
    } else {
      await ref
          .read(todoListProvider.notifier)
          .setItemDue(widget.id, due, completed: _completed);
    }
  } catch (e) {
    if (!mounted) return;
    setState(() => _dueAt = previous);
    showTaskError(context, e);
  }
}
```

`TimeOfDay.fromDateTime(current ?? noon)` — current is a DateTime; fromDateTime extracts hour/minute ✓.

Note: showDatePicker returns a DateTime at local midnight (date-only) ✓.

_toggle in TaskSheet:
```dart
Future<void> _toggle() async {
  setState(() => _completed = !_completed);
  try {
    final hook = widget.onToggle;
    if (hook != null) {
      await hook(_completed);
    } else {
      await ref.read(todoListProvider.notifier).markItemAs(_completed, widget.id);
    }
  } catch (e) {
    if (!mounted) return;
    setState(() => _completed = !_completed);
    showTaskError(context, e);
  }
}
```

_saveEdit:
```dart
Future<void> _saveEdit() async {
  final newText = _controller.text;
  setState(() { _text = newText; _editing = false; });
  try {
    final hook = widget.onEdit;
    if (hook != null) {
      await hook(newText);
    } else {
      await ref.read(todoListProvider.notifier).editItem(newText, widget.id);
    }
  } catch (e) {
    if (mounted) showTaskError(context, e);
  }
}
```

_delete:
```dart
Future<void> _delete() async {
  try {
    final hook = widget.onDelete;
    if (hook != null) {
      await hook();
    } else {
      await ref.read(todoListProvider.notifier).deleteItem(widget.id);
    }
    if (mounted) Navigator.pop(context);
  } catch (e) {
    if (mounted) showTaskError(context, e);
  }
}
```
